{"batchcomplete":true,"query":{"pages":[{"pageid":60744210,"ns":0,"title":"UFC Fight Night: Maia vs. Askren","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts event in 2019}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name= UFC Fight Night: Maia vs. Askren\n|image= UFC on ESPN+ 20.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date= {{start date|2019|10|26}}\n|venue= [[Singapore Indoor Stadium]]\n|city= [[Kallang]], Singapore\n|attendance=7,155{{Cite web|url=https://www.lowkickmma.com/ufc-singapore-bonuses-attendance-gate/|title=UFC Singapore Bonuses, Attendance & Gate|author= Jon Fuentes|date=2019-10-26|publisher=lowkicmma.com|language=en-US|access-date=2019-10-26}}\n|gate=\n|previousevent=[[UFC on ESPN: Reyes vs. Weidman]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC 244|UFC 244: Masvidal vs Diaz]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Maia vs. Askren''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 162''''' or ''''' UFC on ESPN+ 20''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that took place on October 26, 2019 at the [[Singapore Indoor Stadium]] in [[Kallang]], Singapore.{{Cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/askren-vs-maia-headline-ufcs-return-singapore|title=Askren vs. Maia to headline UFC's return to Singapore|author=Staff|date=2019-08-25|publisher=ufc.com|access-date=2019-08-26}}\n\n== Background ==\nA welterweight bout between former [[UFC Welterweight Championship|UFC Welterweight]] and [[UFC Middleweight Championship|Middleweight Championship]] challenger [[Demian Maia]] and former [[List of Bellator champions#Welterweight Championship|Bellator Welterweight Champion]] and former [[List of ONE Championship champions#Welterweight World Championship|ONE Welterweight Champion]], [[Ben Askren]] headlined the event.\n\nA heavyweight bout between [[Greg Hardy]] and [[Jarjis Danho]] was scheduled for the event. However, Danho was pulled from the event for an undisclosed reason. He was replaced by Ben Sosoli on September 19 and the bout moved to [[UFC on ESPN: Reyes vs. Weidman]] a week earlier.{{Cite web|url=http://www.fightnewsaustralia.com/ben-sosoli-set-to-fight-greg-hardy-at-ufc-boston/|title=Ben Sosoli set to fight Greg Hardy at UFC Boston|author=Staff|publisher=fightnewsaustralia.com|date=2019-09-20|access-date=2019-09-20}}\n\n[[Yan Xiaonan]] was scheduled to face [[Ashley Yoder]] at the event. However, Yan pulled out of the fight in late-September citing a foot injury.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/9/25/20883370/yan-xiaonan-out-ufc-singapore-ashley-yoder-mma|title=Yan Xiaonan out of UFC Singapore bout with Ashley Yoder|author=Guilherme Cruz|publisher=mmafighting.com|date=2019-09-25|access-date=2019-09-25}} She was replaced by [[Randa Markos]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/09/yan-xiaonan-out-randa-markos-in-against-ashley-yoder-ufc-singapore|title=With Yan Xiaonan out, Randa Markos steps in to fight Ashley Yoder at UFC on ESPN+ 20|author=Nolan King and Farah Hannoun|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|date=2019-09-26|access-date=2019-09-26}}\n\n[[Ian Heinisch]] was scheduled to face [[Brad Tavares]] at the event. However, Heinisch was removed from the pairing in early October for undisclosed reasons. In turn, Tavares was removed from the event entirely and rescheduled to face [[Edmen Shahbazyan]] a week later at [[UFC 244]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/10/brad-tavares-moved-ufc-244-edmen-shahbazyan-middleweight|title=Brad Tavares moved to UFC 244 for matchup with unbeaten Edmen Shahbazyan|author=Mike Bohn and Farah Hannoun|date=2019-10-02|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-10-02}}\n\n[[Karol Rosa]] was scheduled to face [[Julia Avila]] at the event. However, Rosa pulled out of the fight in mid-October citing a knee injury and the bout was scrapped.{{Cite web|url=https://globoesporte.globo.com/es/noticia/karol-rosa-lesiona-o-joelho-e-esta-fora-da-luta-com-julia-avila-no-ufc-singapura.ghtml|title=Karol Rosa injures her knee and is out of the fight with Julia Avila at UFC Singapore|author=Staff|publisher=globoesporte.globo.com|date=2019-10-16|access-date=2019-10-16}} {{in lang|pt}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (ESPN+)}} UFC_244_Poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date={{start date|2019|11|02}}\n|venue=[[Madison Square Garden]]\n|city= [[New York City]], [[New York (state)|New York]], United States\n|attendance=20,143{{Cite web|url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/trentreinsmith/2019/11/03/ufc-244-delivers-second-best-gate-and-attendance-for-ufc-event-in-new-york/|title=UFC 244 Delivers Askren]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Zabit vs. Kattar]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 244: Masvidal vs. Diaz''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] pay-per-view event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that took place on November 2, 2019, at [[Madison Square Garden]] in [[New York City]], [[New York (state)|New York]], United States.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/7/8/20686317/ufc-fourth-quarter-schedule-2019|title=UFC announces fourth quarter schedule for 2019|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2019-07-08|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-07-08}} It was the UFC's 500th live event.{{cite web |url=https://www.fightbookmma.com/ufc-will-hold-its-500th-event-at-madison-square-garden-headlined-by-jorge-masvidal-vs-nate-diaz/|title=UFC will hold its 500th event at Madison Square Garden headlined by Jorge Masvidal vs Nate Diaz|publisher=fightbookmma.com|author=Staff|date=2019-09-07|access-date=2019-10-26}}\n\n==Background==\n\n===Card changes and main event jeopardy===\nA welterweight bout between [[Jorge Masvidal]] and former [[UFC Lightweight Championship]] challenger and [[The Ultimate Fighter 5]] lightweight winner [[Nate Diaz]] headlined the event.{{Cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/ufc/story/_/id/27538140/diaz-masvidal-headline-ufc-244-nov-2|title=Diaz-Masvidal to headline UFC 244 on Nov. 2|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2019-09-07|work=espn.com|access-date=2019-09-07}} After his [[UFC 241]] win against former lightweight champion [[Anthony Pettis]], Diaz proclaimed himself the \"baddest [[motherfucker]] in the game\" and issued a challenge to Masvidal. When the fight was scheduled, [[Dana White]] announced that a celebratory \"baddest motherfucker\" belt, embossed \"BMF,\" would be awarded to the winner.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/09/ufc-244-nate-diaz-jorge-masvidal-dana-white-bmf-belt-winner|title=Dana White confirms Nate Diaz-Jorge Masvidal winner will get 'BMF' belt|author=Dave Doyle and Matt Erickson|date=2019-09-19|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-09-20}} However, on October 24, Diaz tweeted a statement saying he would not be competing due to an adverse drug test finding, which put the bout in jeopardy.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/10/24/20931227/nate-diaz-claims-hes-failed-drug-test-wont-fight-at-ufc-244|title=Nate Diaz claims he's failed drug test, won't fight at UFC 244|author=Steve Marracco|date=2019-10-24|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-10-24}} On October 26, the UFC announced that Diaz had been cleared to compete, as his positive test had traces of LGD-4033, or [[Ligandrol]], a selective androgen receptor modulator which was known to be linked to a tainted batch of organic, vegan multivitamins.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/10/ufc-244-main-event-of-jorge-masvidal-vs-nate-diaz-still-on|title=UFC 244 main event of Jorge Masvidal vs. Nate Diaz still on|author=Dave Doyle and John Morgan|date=2019-10-26|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-10-26}}\n\n[[Krzysztof Jotko]] was scheduled to face [[Edmen Shahbazyan]] at the event. However, Jotko was removed from the card in early October for undisclosed reasons and replaced by [[Brad Tavares]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/10/brad-tavares-moved-ufc-244-edmen-shahbazyan-middleweight|title=Brad Tavares moved to UFC 244 for matchup with unbeaten Edmen Shahbazyan|author=Mike Bohn and Farah Hannoun|date=2019-10-02|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-10-02}}\n\n===Weigh-ins===\nAt the weigh-ins, former [[Invicta Fighting Championships#Flyweight Championship|Invicta FC Flyweight Champion]] [[Jennifer Maia]] weighed in at 127.2 pounds, 1.2 pounds over the flyweight non-title fight limit of 126 pounds. The bout proceeded at a [[catchweight]] and Maia was fined 25% of her purse, which went to her opponent [[Katlyn Chookagian]].{{Cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/UFC-244-Weighin-Results-Main-Event-Set-Jennifer-Maia-Misses-Weight-164805|title=UFC 244 weigh-in results: main event set; Jennifer Maia misses weight|author=Cole Shelton|publisher=sherdog.com|date=2019-11-02|access-date=2019-11-02}}\n\nPrior to the ceremonial weigh-ins, the [[New York State Athletic Commission]] (NYSAC) issued a rare statement in which it was announced that they would be pursuing disciplinary action against [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Jones vs. Team Sonnen]] middleweight winner and former interim title challenger [[Kelvin Gastelum]]. In the statement, the Athletic Commission stated that following a review of Gastelum's weigh-in video they \"determined that Mr. Gastelum made contact with another person while on the scale, a violation of the weigh in policy. In light of this violation, the Commission will pursue disciplinary action. At this time, the official weight determination will not be disturbed, and Mr. Gastelum will not be disqualified from competing in UFC 244.\"{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/11/1/20944287/nysac-decides-to-pursue-disciplinary-action-against-kelvin-gastelum-but-ufc-244-will-go-on|title=NYSAC decides to pursue 'disciplinary action' against Kelvin Gastelum, but UFC 244 bout will go on|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2019-11-01|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-11-01}}\n\n===Fines===\nOn November 8, it was announced that [[Corey Anderson (fighter)|Corey Anderson]] had been fined $10,000 by the NYSAC for \"unsportsmanlike and disorderly conduct\" for taunting and screaming at [[Johnny Walker (fighter)|Johnny Walker]] and shoving the referee away during his post-fight celebration. Kelvin Gastelum was also fined $1,000 for making physical contact with his coach [[Rafael Cordeiro]] while on the scale at the weigh-ins.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/11/8/20955654/nysac-fines-kelvin-gastelum-rafael-cordeiro-and-corey-anderson-for-ufc-244-actions|title=NYSAC fines Kelvin Gastelum, Rafael Cordeiro and Corey Anderson for UFC 244 actions|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2019-11-08|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-11-09}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card}}\n{{MMAevent Heavyweight Championship|UFC Heavyweight Champion]] [[Junior dos Santos]] and former [[List of Bellator champions#Heavyweight Championship|Bellator Heavyweight Champion]] [[Alexander Volkov (fighter)|Alexander Volkov]] was scheduled to serve as the event headliner.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/09/ufc-moscow-junior-dos-santos-vs-alexander-volkov-headliner-booked|title=Junior Dos Santos vs. Alexander Volkov set to headline UFC on ESPN+ 21|date=2019-09-07|author=Nolan King|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-09-07}} However, dos Santos pulled out of the matchup on October 22, citing a [[Bacterial Infections|bacterial infection]] in his leg.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/10/22/20926520/junior-do-santos-hospitalized-ufc-moscow-fight-alexander-volkov-mma|title=Junior dos Santos hospitalized, out of UFC Moscow fight with Alexander Volkov|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2019-10-22|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-10-22}} [[Greg Hardy]] was announced as the replacement on the same day, moving this bout down into the co-main event slot.{{Cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/27903715/greg-hardy-replace-junior-dos-santos-november-carda|title=Greg Hardy to replace Junior dos Santos on November card|author=Brett Okamoto|date=2019-10-22|work=espn.com|access-date=2019-10-22}}\n\nA featherweight bout between [[Zabit Magomedsharipov]] and [[Calvin Kattar]] was scheduled for [[UFC on ESPN: Reyes vs. Weidman]]. However, promotion officials then elected to instead reschedule the pairing for this event.{{Cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Zabit-Magomedsharipov-Calvin-Kattar-Rescheduled-for-UFC-Fight-Night-163-in-Moscow-161887|title=Zabit Magomedsharipov, Calvin Kattar rescheduled for UFC Fight Night 163 in Moscow|author=Tristen Critchfield|publisher=sherdog.com|date=2019-09-17|access-date=2019-09-17}} In turn, after a change up in participants in the original main event, the pairing was elevated to serve as a three round headliner.\n\n[[Vinc Pichel]] was linked to a bout with [[Alexander Yakovlev (fighter)|Alexander Yakovlev]] at the event. However, Pichel pulled out of the fight on October 24, citing an undisclosed injury and was replaced by [[Roosevelt Roberts]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/roosevelt-roberts-vervangt-vinc-pichel-tegen-alexander-yakovlev-tijdens-ufc-moskou/|title=Roosevelt Roberts replaces Vinc Pichel against Alexander Yakovlev during UFC Moscow|author=Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2019-10-24|access-date=2019-10-24}} {{in lang|nl}}\n\nA light heavyweight bout between [[Gadzhimurad Antigulov]] and [[Ed Herman]] was scheduled for the event. However, Antigulov was pulled from the bout for an undisclosed reason and replaced by [[Khadis Ibragimov]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/breaking-khadis-ibragimov-pakt-short-notice-partij-tegen-ed-herman-in-moskou/|title=BREAKING: Khadis Ibragimov takes short notice against Ed Herman in description|UFC mixed martial arts event in 2019}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name= UFC Fight Night: Błachowicz vs. Jacaré\n|image= UFC_on_ESPN+_22.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting web|url=https://www.lowkickmma.com/ufc-sao-paulo-bonuses-attendance-gate/amp/|title=UFC São Paulo bonuses, attendance & gate|date=2019-11-16|publisher=lowkickmma.com|author=Jon Fuentes|access-date=2019-11-16}}\n|gate=\n|previousevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Zabit vs. Kattar]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC on ESPN: Overeem vs. Rozenstruik]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Błachowicz vs. Jacaré''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 164''''' and '''''UFC on ESPN+ 22''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that took place on November 16, 2019 at [[Ginásio do Ibirapuera]] in [[São Paulo]], Brazil.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/7/8/20686317/ufc-fourth-quarter-schedule-2019|title=UFC announces fourth quarter Gerbasi|date=2019-09-13|access-date=2019-09-13}}\n\n==Background==\nA light heavyweight bout between former [[Konfrontacja Sztuk Walki#Light heavyweight championship|KSW Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Jan Błachowicz]] and former [[List of Strikeforce champions#Middleweight Championship|Strikeforce Middleweight Champion]] [[Ronaldo Souza]] served as the event headliner.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/09/ronaldo-jacare-souza-vs-jan-blachowicz-headlines-ufc-espn-plus-22|title=Ronaldo 'Jacare' Souza vs. Jan Blachowicz headlines UFC on ESPN+ 22|date=2019-09-10|author=Dave Doyle|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-09-10}}\n\nA light heavyweight bout between [[Antônio Rogério Nogueira]] and [[Trevor Smith (fighter)|Trevor Smith]] was scheduled for the event.{{Cite web|url=https://sportv.globo.com/site/combate/noticia/ultimate-encaminha-luta-entre-rogerio-minotouro-e-trevor-smith-para-o-ufc-sao-paulo.ghtml|title=Ultimate gets closer to book fight between Rogério Minotouro and Trevor Smith to UFC São Paulo|publisher=sportv.globo.com|author=Marcelo Barone|date=14 August 2019 |access-date=2019-09-25}} {{in lang|pt}} However, Nogueira was forced to pull out of the event due to injury, resulting in the cancellation of the bout. Smith was then expected to face [[Makhmud Muradov]] at [[UFC on ESPN: Overeem vs. Rozenstruik]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/ongeslagen-alonzo-menifield-treft-trevor-smith-tijdens-ufc-washington/|title=Undefeated Alonzo Menifield meets Trevor Smith during UFC Washington|publisher=mmadna.nl|author=Redactie|date=24 September 2019|access-date=2019-09-25}} {{in lang|nl}} \n\n[[Leah Letson]] was expected to face Duda Santana at the event. However, Letson was removed from the bout in late September for undisclosed reasons.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/9/26/20885172/amanda-lemos-replacing-leah-letson-against-duda-santana-at-ufc-sao-paolo|title=Leah Letson out of UFC Sao Paulo bout against Duda Santana|date=2019-09-26|author=Guilherme Cruz|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-09-26}} Instead, Santana was expected to face promotional newcomer [[Tracy Cortez]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/09/ufc-sao-paulo-tracy-cortez-debut-vs-duda-santana|title=After earning contract on DWCS, Tracy Cortez to debut vs. Duda Santana at UFC on ESPN+ 22|date=2019-09-27|author=Nolan King and John Morgan|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-09-27}} However, Santana then withdrew from the bout and was replaced by [[Vanessa Melo]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/10/duda-santana-out-tracy-cortez-vs-vanessa-melo-ufc-sao-paulo|title = With Duda Santana out, Tracy Cortez now meets Vanessa Melo at UFC Sao Paulo|date = 29 October 2019}}\n\n[[Kevin Holland]] was scheduled to face promotional newcomer [[Antônio Arroyo]] at the event. However, promotion officials elected to remove Holland from the bout in favor of a matchup a month earlier at [[UFC on ESPN: Reyes vs. Weidman]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/09/kevin-holland-replacement-vs-brendan-allen-middleweight-ufc-on-espn-6|title=Kevin Holland shifts from Brazil to Boston, steps in vs. Brendan Allen at UFC on ESPN 6|date=2019-09-30|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Nolan King|access-date=2019-10-01}} In turn, [[Trevin Giles]], then [[Alessio Di Chirico]] were briefly linked as replacements to face Arroyo.{{Cite web|url=https://sportv.globo.com/site/combate/noticia/ultimate-escala-trevin-giles-como-substituto-de-kevin-holland-na-luta-contra-antonio-arroyo-em-sp.ghtml|title=UFC sets Trevin Giles as Kevin Holland's replacement in the fight against Antonio Arroyo in São Paulo|author=Marcelo Barone|publisher=sportv.globo.com|date=2019-10-02|access-date=2019-10-03}} {{in lang|pt}}{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/alessio-di-chirico-pakt-short-notice-gevecht-tegen-antonio-arroyo-tijdens-ufc-sao-paulo/|title=Alessio Di Chirico tackles short notice fight against Antonio Arroyo during UFC São Paulo|author=Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2019-10-10|access-date=2019-10-11}} {{in lang|nl}} Instead, Arroyo fought fellow newcomer [[André Muniz]].{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/10/24/20930364/andre-muniz-fight-antonio-arroyo-ufc-sao-paulo-mma|title=Andre Muniz steps in to fight Antonio Arroyo at UFC Sao Paulo|date=2019-10-24|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|access-date=2019-10-24}}\n\nA middleweight bout between [[Markus Perez]] and [[Jack Marshman]] was scheduled for the event. However, Marshman was pulled from the event for an undisclosed reason and replaced by [[Wellington Turman]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/wellington-turman-vervangt-jack-marshman-tegen-markus-perez-tijdens-ufc-sao-paulo/|title=Wellington Turman replaces Jack Marshman against Markus Perez during UFC São Paulo|author= Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2019-10-12|access-date=2019-10-12}} {{in lang|nl}}\n\n[[Sam Alvey]] was scheduled to face former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship|UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Maurício Rua]] at the event. However, Alvey was removed from the fight on October 25 due to a broken hand.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/10/sam-alvey-broken-hand-out-shogun-rua-fight-ufc-on-espn-plus-22|title=Sam Alvey: Broken hand behind 'heartbreaking' withdrawal vs. 'Shogun' Rua at UFC on ESPN+ 22|date=2019-10-27|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2019-10-28}} Instead, Rua fought [[Paul Craig]].{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/10/29/20938877/shogun-rua-paul-craig-ufc-sao-paulo-mma|title='Shogun' Rua vs. Paul Craig announced for UFC Sao Paulo|author=Guilherme |date=2019-10-29|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-10-30}}\n\nA flyweight bout between former [[Konfrontacja_Sztuk_Walki#Women's flyweight championship|KSW Women's Flyweight Champion]] [[Ariane Lipski]] and [[Priscila Cachoeira]] was scheduled to take place at the event. However, upon testing positive for a [[diuretic]] and being pulled from the event, Cachoeira was replaced by [[Veronica Macedo]].{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/11/6/20952536/veronica-macedo-steps-in-to-fight-ariane-lipski-at-ufc-sao-paulo|title=Veronica Macedo steps in to fight Ariane Lipski at UFC Sao Paulo|author=Alexander K Lee|date=2019-11-06|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-11-07}} In turn, Macedo was not cleared to fight by CABMMA due to severe headaches one day before the event and she was replaced by newcomer Isabella de Pádua.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/11/ariane-lipski-vs-isabella-de-padua-ufc-sao-paulo-veronica-macedo-withdraws|title=Ariane Lipski gets new opponent for UFC Sao Paulo after Veronica Macedo forced to withdraw|date=2019-11-15|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Farah Hannoun|access-date=2019-11-15}} At the weigh-ins, de Pádua weighed in at 130.5 pounds, 4.5 pounds over the flyweight non-title fight limit of 126 pounds. The bout proceeded at a catchweight. As a result of missing weight, de Pádua was fined 30% of her purse, which went to Lipski.{{Cite web|url=https://cagesidepress.com/2019/11/15/ufc-sao-paulo-weigh-in-results/|title=UFC São Paulo weigh-in results: main event official|author=Staff|date=2019-11-15|publisher=cagesidepress.com|access-date=2019-11-15}}\n\nAt the weigh-ins, [[Tracy Cortez]] and her opponent [[Vanessa Melo]] both missed weight for their fight. Cortez and Melo both weighed in at 136.5 pounds, 0.5 pounds over the bantamweight non-title fight limit of 136 pounds. The bout would initially proceed at a catchweight and no fine was issued due to identical misses. However, later on the same day, CABMMA executive director Cristiano Sampaio announced that due to an error of the scale used at the weigh-ins, which was set was 0.7 pounds above the official scale, Cortez and Melo were officially clear from missing weight and thus the bout proceeded at bantamweight.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/11/15/20966934/commission-tracy-cortez-vanessa-melo-did-not-miss-weight-for-ufc-sao-paulo-error-due-to-scale-issue|title=Commission: Tracy Cortez, Vanessa Melo did not miss weight for UFC São Paulo, error due to scale issue|date=2019-11-15|publisher=mmafghting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|access-date=2019-11-16}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that took place on December 7, 2019 at [[Capital One Arena]] in [[Washington, D.C.]], United States.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/7/8/20686317/ufc-fourth-quarter-schedule-2019|title=UFC announces fourth quarter schedule for 2019|author=Guilherme web|url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/sports/2019/07/07/ufc-return-washington-december-show-capital-one-arena/?noredirect=on|title=UFC to return to Washington for December event at Capital One Arena|publisher=washingtonpost.com|author=Rick Maese|date=2019-07-07|access-date=2019-07-26}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was the second that the promotion had contested in the American capital, following [[UFC Live: Cruz vs. Johnson]] in October 2011.\n\nThe event was also dubbed 'Fight Like Hell Night' and held in honor of the late [[ESPN]] broadcaster [[Stuart Scott]], who died from cancer in 2015.{{Cite web|url=https://www.sportskeeda.com/mma/ufc-on-espn-7-results-alistair-overeem-suffers-one-of-the-worst-looking-injuries-in-mma-history-two-big-fights-end-in-draws|title=UFC on ESPN 7 Results: Alistair Overeem suffers one of the worst looking injuries in MMA history, two big fights end in draws|date=2019-12-08|website=www.sportskeeda.com|language=en-us|access-date=2019-12-09}}{{Cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mens-college-basketball/story/_/id/28153329/ufc-fight-hell-night-celebrates-stuart-scott|title=UFC Fight Like Hell Night: Donate now to the V Foundation|date=2019-12-02|website=ESPN.com|language=en|access-date=2019-12-09}}\n\nA heavyweight bout between former [[List of K-1 champions|2010 K-1 World Grand Prix Champion]], [[List of Strikeforce champions#Heavyweight Championship|Strikeforce Heavyweight Champion]] and [[UFC Heavyweight Championship]] challenger [[Alistair Overeem]] and [[Walt Harris (fighter)|Walt Harris]] was expected to serve as the event headliner.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/08/ufc-washington-dc-alistair-overeem-vs-walt-harris-headlines|title=Alistair Overeem vs. Walt Harris headlines UFC on ESPN 7|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2019-08-13|access-date=2019-08-14}}{{Cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/overeem-vs-harris-headline-ufc-washington-dc|title=Overeem vs. Harris to headline UFC Washington D.C.|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2019-08-14|access-date=2019-08-15}} However on November 1, Harris withdrew from the event due to the ongoing search for his missing stepdaughter, whose remains were found a month after her disappearance.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/11/walt-harris-out-ufc-on-espn-7-dc-main-event-alistair-overeem/amp|title=Walt Harris withdraws from UFC on ESPN 7 headliner vs. Alistair Overeem|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2019-11-01|access-date=2019-11-01}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }}{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmamania.com/2019/11/27/20986205/aniah-blanchard-confirmed-dead-following-autopsy-report|title=Aniah Blanchard confirmed dead following autopsy report|publisher=mmamania.com|author=Adam Guillen Jr.|date=2019-11-27|access-date=2019-12-06}} He was replaced by [[Jairzinho Rozenstruik]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/11/jair-rozenstruik-meets-alistair-overeem-ufc-on-espn-7-headliner-walt-harris-out/amp|title=Jair Rozenstruik meets Alistair Overeem in UFC on ESPN 7 headliner|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2019-11-04|access-date=2019-11-04}}\n\nA women's strawweight bout between former [[UFC Women's Strawweight Championship]] challenger [[Cláudia Gadelha]] and [[Cynthia Calvillo]] was scheduled for the event. However on October 22, it was announced that Gadelha was forced to withdraw from the bout due to a broken finger and a torn ligament.{{Cite web|url=https://www.bjpenn.com/mma-news/ufc/claudia-gadelha-withdraws-from-cynthia-calvillo-fight-angela-hill-offers-to-fill-in/|title=Claudia Gadelha withdraws from Cynthia Calvillo fight, Angela Hill offers to fill in|author=Cole Shelton|date=2019-10-23|publisher= bjpenn.com|access-date=2019-10-23}} She was replaced by [[Marina Rodriguez]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/marina-rodriguez-vervangt-claudia-gadelha-tegen-cynthia-calvillo-tijdens-ufc-washington/|title=Marina Rodriguez replaces Claudia Gadelha against Cynthia Calvillo during UFC Washington|author=Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2019-10-24|access-date=2019-10-24}} {{in lang|nl}}\n\nA women's strawweight bout between [[Cortney Casey]] and former [[Invicta Fighting Championships|Invicta FC Strawweight Champion]] [[Virna Jandiroba]] was scheduled for the event.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/08/cortney-casey-vs-virna-jandiroba-ufc-on-espn-7-washington-dc/amp|title=Cortney Casey vs. Virna Jandiroba added to UFC on ESPN 7 in Washington, D.C.|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Farah Hannoun|date=2019-09-14|access-date=2019-11-01|archive-date=2019-09-27|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190927040455/https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/08/cortney-casey-vs-virna-jandiroba-ufc-on-espn-7-washington-dc/amp|url-status=dead}} However, Casey pulled out on October 31 due to undisclosed reasons. She was replaced by another former Invicta FC Strawweight Champion [[Lívia Renata Souza]].{{cite news|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/platform/amp/2019/10/31/20942132/livinha-souza-replaces-cortney-casey-virna-jandiroba-ufc-espn-7-washington|title=Livinha Souza replaces Cortney Casey against Virna Jandiroba at UFC on ESPN 7|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2019-10-31|access-date=2019-11-01|archive-date=2019-11-01|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191101115145/https://www.mmafighting.com/platform/amp/2019/10/31/20942132/livinha-souza-replaces-cortney-casey-virna-jandiroba-ufc-espn-7-washington|url-status=dead}} In turn, Souza pulled out of the fight a few weeks later and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Mallory Martin]].{{cite news|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/11/26/20983770/mallory-martin-replaces-livinha-souza-virna-jandiroba-ufc-espn|title=Mallory Martin replaces injured Livinha Souza against Virna Jandiroba at UFC on ESPN 7|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2019-11-26|access-date=2019-11-26}}\n\nA middleweight bout between [[Alonzo Menifield]] and [[Trevor Smith (fighter)|Trevor Smith]] was scheduled for the event. However on November 10, it was reported that Menifield pulled out of the event for an undisclosed reason and was replaced by [[Makhmud Muradov]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/wederom-short-notice-partij-voor-makhmud-muradov-ditmaal-tegen-trevor-smith-in-washington/|title=Again short notice for Makhmud Muradov, this time against Trevor Smith in Washington|author=Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2019-11-09|access-date=2019-11-09}} {{in lang|nl}}\n\n[[Mickey Gall]] was expected to face former [[List of WEC champions#Welterweight Championship|WEC Welterweight Champion]] and interim [[UFC Welterweight Champion]] [[Carlos Condit]] at the event.{{cite news|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/platform/amp/2019/8/28/20837430/carlos-condit-vs-mickey-gall-headed-to-ufc-on-espn-7-in-d-c|title=Carlos Condit vs. Mickey Gall headed to UFC on ESPN 7 in D.C.|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Damon Martin|date=2019-09-28|access-date=2019-11-14|archive-date=2019-12-13|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191213142139/https://www.mmafighting.com/platform/amp/2019/8/28/20837430/carlos-condit-vs-mickey-gall-headed-to-ufc-on-espn-7-in-d-c|url-status=dead}} However, Condit pulled out on November 13 due to a detached retina and the bout was subsequently canceled.{{Cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Detached-Retina-Forces-Carlos-Condit-Out-of-UFC-on-ESPN-7-Matchup-with-Mickey-Gall-165785|title=Detached retina forces Carlos Condit out of UFC on ESPN 7 matchup with Mickey Gall|publisher=sherdog.com|author=Tristen Critchfield|date=2019-11-14|access-date=2019-11-14}}{{Cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Mickey-Gall-Pulled-from-UFC-on-ESPN-7-Card-Following-Carlos-Condit-Injury-166605|title=Mickey Gall pulled from UFC on ESPN 7 card following Carlos Condit injury|author=Tristen Critchefield|publisher=sherdog.com|date=2019-11-27|access-date=2019-11-27}}\n\nA featherweight bout between [[Chris Fishgold]] and [[Billy Quarantillo]] was scheduled for the event. However, Fishgold withdrew from the bout on November 27 for an undisclosed reason.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/11/chris-fishgold-out-ufc-on-espn-7-washington-vs-billy-quarantillo|title=Chris Fishgold out of UFC on ESPN 7 fight against Billy Quarantillo|date=2019-11-26|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Nolan King|access-date=2019-11-27}} He was replaced by Jacob Kilburn.{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/jacob-kilburn-pakt-short-notice-partij-tegen-billy-quarantillo-tijdens-ufc-washington/|title=Jacob Kilburn takes a short notice game against Billy Quarantillo during UFC Washington|publisher=mmadna.nl|author=Redactie|date=2019-11-29|access-date=2019-11-29}} {{in lang|nl}}\n\nAt the weigh-ins, [[Cynthia Calvillo]] and Matt Sayles missed the required weight for their respective fights. Calvillo weighed in at 120.5 pounds, 4.5 pounds over the strawweight non-title fight limit of 116 pounds. Sayles weighed in at 148.5 pounds, 2.5 pounds over the featherweight non-title fight limit of 146 pounds. Both bouts were held at a catchweight. Calvillo and Sayles were fined 30% and 20% of their purses, which went to their opponents Rodriguez and Mitchell.{{Cite Overeem-Rozenstruik official, but Calvillo misses by 4.5 pounds|date=2019-12-06|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Staff|access-date=2019-12-06}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent December 14, 2019, at [[T-Mobile Arena]] in [[Paradise, Nevada]], part of the [[Las Vegas–Paradise, NV MSA|Las Vegas Metropolitan Area]], United States.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/7/8/20686317/ufc-fourth-quarter-schedule-2019|title=UFC announces fourth quarter schedule for 2019|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2019-07-08|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-07-08}}{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/de-ufc-kondigt-evenementen-aan-voor-moskou-singapore-busan-en-meer/|title=The UFC announces events for Moscow, Singapore, Busan and more|author=Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2019-07-08|access-date=2019-07-08}} {{in lang|nl}}\n\n==Background==\nA [[UFC Welterweight Championship]] bout between the current champion and [[The Ultimate Fighter: American Top Team vs. Blackzilians]] winner [[Kamaru Usman]] and former interim champion [[Colby Covington]] served as the event headliner.{{Cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/kamaru-usman-vs-colby-covington-headline-ufc-245|title=Kamaru Usman vs. Colby Covington to headline UFC 245|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2019-10-01|publisher=ufc.com|access-date=2019-10-02}}\n\nA [[UFC Featherweight Championship]] bout between the current champion [[Max Holloway]] and [[Alexander Volkanovski]] took place as the co-main event.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/9/21/20877616/max-holloway-vs-alexander-volkanovski-set-for-ufc-245|title=Max Holloway vs. Alexander Volkanovski set for UFC 245|author=Alexander K. Lee|date=2019-09-21|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-09-22}}\n\nA [[UFC Women's Bantamweight Championship]] bout between the current champion [[Amanda Nunes]] (also the current [[UFC Women's Featherweight Championship|UFC Women's Featherweight Champion]]) and the inaugural featherweight champion [[Germaine de Randamie]] also took place at the event.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/08/ufc-245-amanda-nunes-vs-germaine-de-randamie-bantamweight-title-fight-booked|title=Amanda Nunes to defend bantamweight title vs. Germaine de Randamie at UFC 245|author=Nolan King|date=2019-08-17|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-08-19}} The pairing previously met in November 2013 at [[UFC: Fight for the Troops 3]], with Nunes winning the encounter via TKO in the first round.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/ufc-fight-night-31-results-photos-germaine-de-randamie-vs-amanda-nunes/|title=UFC Fight Night 31 results, photos: Amanda Nunes pounds out Germaine De Randamie|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2013-11-06|access-date=2019-08-19|archive-date=2017-06-02|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170602030751/http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/ufc-fight-night-31-results-photos-germaine-de-randamie-vs-amanda-nunes|url-status=dead}}\n\n[[Santiago Ponzinibbio]] was expected to face former welterweight champion [[Robbie Lawler]] at the event. However, Ponzinibbio pulled out of the fight on October 12 citing a [[staph infection]]. Despite having over two months before the event, promotion officials elected to remove Lawler from the card entirely instead of having him face a replacement.{{cite web|url=https://www.bloodyelbow.com/2019/10/12/20910907/santiago-ponzinibbio-out-of-ufc-245-fight-with-robbie-lawler-welterweight-mma-news|title=Santiago Ponzinibbio out of UFC 245 fight vs. Robbie Lawler|publisher=bloodyelbow.com|author=Nick Baldwin|date=2019-10-12|access-date=2019-10-12|archive-date=2019-12-09|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191209073944/https://www.bloodyelbow.com/2019/10/12/20910907/santiago-ponzinibbio-out-of-ufc-245-fight-with-robbie-lawler-welterweight-mma-news|url-status=dead}}\n\n[[Sergio Pettis]] was briefly linked to a bout with [[Kai Kara-France]] at the event.{{cite web|url=https://www.stuff.co.nz/sport/combat-sports/116172203/kiwi-kai-karafrance-books-fight-against-sergio-pettis-at-ufc-245-in-las-vegas|title=Kiwi Kai Kara-France books fight against Sergio Pettis at UFC 245 in Las Vegas|publisher=stuff.co.nz|author=Marvin France|date=2019-09-29|access-date=2019-10-04}} However, Pettis revealed in early October that he was entertaining offers from other promotions after the completion of his previous contract and did not currently have a fight lined up with the promotion.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmamania.com/2019/10/3/20897222/free-agency-puts-sergio-pettis-vs-kai-kara-france-hold-ufc-245|title=Free agency puts Sergio Pettis vs Kai Kara-France 'on hold' for UFC 245|publisher=mmamania.com|author=Jesse Holland|date=2019-10-03|access-date=2019-10-04}} Kara-France ultimately faced [[Brandon Moreno]].{{cite news|url=https://www.nzherald.co.nz/sport/news/article.cfm?c_id=4&objectid=12273775|title= Kai Kara-France to fight Brandon Moreno at UFC 245, Sergio Pettis now testing free agency|publisher=nzherald.co.nz|author=Christopher Rieve|date=2019-10-04|access-date=2019-10-04}} \n\nAt the weigh-ins, former [[UFC Women's Flyweight Championship]] challenger [[Jessica Eye]] failed to make weight, coming in at 131 pounds, five pounds over the flyweight non-title fight limit of 126 pounds. She was fined 30% of her fight purse, which went to her opponent [[Viviane Araújo (fighter)|Viviane Araújo]] and the bout proceeded at a catchweight.{{cite web|url=https://www.lowkickmma.com/ufc-245-weigh-in-results-all-but-one-fighter-makes-weight/|title=UFC 245 weigh-in results: all but one fighter makes weight|publisher=lowkickmma.com|author=Jon Petr Yan''' and '''Irene Aldana'''\n\n==Reported payout==\nThe following is the reported payout to the fighters as reported to the [[Nevada State Athletic Commission]]. It does not include sponsor money and also does not include the UFC's traditional \"fight night\" bonuses. The total disclosed payout for the event was $4,084,000.{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/12/ufc-245-salaries-kamaru-usman-colby-covington-top-list-disclosed-paydays|title=UFC 245 salaries: Kamaru Usman, Colby Covington top list of disclosed paydays|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Nolan King|date=2019-12-16}}\n* '''Kamaru Usman:''' $500,000 (no win bonus) def. Colby Covington: $500,000\n* '''Alexander Volkanovski:''' $250,000 (no win bonus) def. Max Holloway: $350,000\n* '''Amanda Nunes:''' $450,000 (includes $100,000 win bonus) def. Germaine de Randamie: $100,000\n* '''Marlon Moraes:''' $220,000 (includes $110,000 win bonus) def. José Aldo: $400,000\n* '''Petr Yan:''' $132,000 (includes $66,000 win bonus) def. Urijah Faber: $250,000\n* '''Geoff Neal:''' $62,000 (includes $31,000 win bonus) def. Mike Perry: $90,000 \n* '''Irene Aldana:''' $80,000 (includes $40,000 win bonus) def. Ketlen Vieira: $33,000\n* '''Omari Akhmedov:''' $110,000 (includes $55,000 win bonus) def. Ian Heinisch: $25,000 \n* '''Matt Brown:''' $160,000 (includes $80,000 win bonus) def. Ben Saunders: $35,000\n* '''Chase Hooper:''' $48,000 (includes $24,000 win bonus) def. Daniel Teymur: $18,000\n* '''Brandon Moreno:''' $62,000 (includes $31,000 win bonus) def. Kai Kara-France: $25,000\n* '''Jessica Eye:''' $96,000 (includes $48,000 win bonus) def. Viviane Araújo: $45,000\n* '''Puna Soriano:''' $20,000 (includes $10,000 win bonus) def. Korea|date=2019-12-21|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2019-12-22|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191221173639/https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/12/ufc-on-espn-23-draws-reported-10651-attendance-for-second-event-in-south-korea|archive-date=2019-12-21|url-status=live}}\n|gate=\n|previousevent=[[UFC 245|UFC 245: Usman vs. Covington]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC 246|UFC 246: McGregor vs. Cowboy]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Edgar vs. The Korean Zombie''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 165''''' and '''''UFC on ESPN+ 23''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that took place on December 21, 2019 at [[Sajik Arena]] in [[Busan]], South Korea.{{Cite web|url=https://news.joins.com/article/23517917|title=UFC hosts Korea in four years ... Place is Busan|author=Joong Ang Ilbo|date=2019-07-07|publisher=news.joins.com|access-date=2019-07-08|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190707150415/https://news.joins.com/article/23517917|archive-date=2019-07-07|url-status=live}} {{in lang|ko}}{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/5/12/18616420/ufc-announces-dates-third-fourth-quarter-2019-including-returns-sacramento-san-antonio-mexico-city|title=UFC announces dates for third, fourth quarter of 2019, including returns to Sacramento, San Antonio, Mexico City|publisher=mmafighting.com|date=2019-05-12|author=Newswire|access-date=2019-05-12|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190512183054/https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/5/12/18616420/ufc-announces-dates-third-fourth-quarter-2019-including-returns-sacramento-san-antonio-mexico-city|archive-date=2019-05-12|url-status=live}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event marked the promotion's first visit to Busan and the second in South Korea, after [[UFC Fight Night: Henderson vs. Masvidal]] in November 2015.\n\nA featherweight bout between former [[UFC Featherweight Championship]] challengers [[Brian Ortega]] and [[The Korean Zombie|Chan Sung Jung]] was originally slated as the event headliner.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/09/ufc-korea-brian-ortega-vs-chan-sung-jung-headliner-booked|title=Brian Ortega vs. Chan Sung Jung to headline UFC on ESPN+ 23 in South Korea|author=Nolan King|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|date=2019-09-23|access-date=2019-09-24|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190924082055/https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/09/ufc-korea-brian-ortega-vs-chan-sung-jung-headliner-booked|archive-date=2019-09-24|url-status=live}} However, Ortega pulled out of the fight in early December, citing a knee injury.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/12/5/20996673/brian-ortega-out-of-ufc-busan-main-event-against-korean-zombie-chan-sung-jung|title=Brian Ortega out of UFC Busan main event against 'Korean Zombie' Chan Sung Jung|author=Damon Martin|publisher=mmafighting.com|date=2019-12-05|access-date=2019-12-05|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191205075331/https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/12/5/20996673/brian-ortega-out-of-ufc-busan-main-event-against-korean-zombie-chan-sung-jung|archive-date=2019-12-05|url-status=live}} He was replaced by former [[UFC Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight Champion]] [[Frankie Edgar]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/12/frankie-edgar-replaces-brian-ortega-meets-chan-sung-jung-ufc-on-espn-plus-23-busan-south-korea|title=Frankie Edgar replaces Brian Ortega, meets Chan Sung Jung at UFC on ESPN+ 23|date=2019-12-05|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Mike Bohn|access-date=2019-12-06|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191206004245/https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/12/frankie-edgar-replaces-brian-ortega-meets-chan-sung-jung-ufc-on-espn-plus-23-busan-south-korea|archive-date=2019-12-06|url-status=live}} The two were previously scheduled to meet on November 10, 2018 at [[UFC Fight Night: The Korean Zombie vs. Rodríguez]].{{Cite news|url=http://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/24153056/frankie-edgar-chan-sung-jung-bout-set-ufc-fight-night-nov-10|title=Frankie Edgar-Chan Sung Jung bout set for UFC Fight Night on Nov. 10|work=espn.com|author=Newswire|date=2018-07-20|access-date=2018-07-20|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180721073232/http://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/24153056/frankie-edgar-chan-sung-jung-bout-set-ufc-fight-night-nov-10|archive-date=2018-07-21|url-status=live}} However, Edgar was forced to pull out of that bout after tearing his bicep.{{Cite news|url=https://mmajunkie.com/2018/10/ufc-fight-night-139-frankie-edgar-out-yair-rodriguez-vs-korean-zombie|title=Frankie Edgar out, Yair Rodriguez in for UFC Fight Night 139 headliner vs. 'Korean Zombie'|date=2018-10-27|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2018-10-27|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181027102810/https://mmajunkie.com/2018/10/ufc-fight-night-139-frankie-edgar-out-yair-rodriguez-vs-korean-zombie|archive-date=2018-10-27|url-status=live}}\n\nA flyweight bout between [[Ji Yeon Kim]] and [[Sabina Mazo]] was scheduled for the event. However, it was reported on November 1 that Kim was forced to pull out of the fight due to an undisclosed injury.{{Cite web|url=https://www.fightful.com/mma/report-ji-yeon-kim-ufc-fight-night-busan|title=Report: Ji Yeon Kim Off UFC Fight Night Busan|publisher=fightful.com|author=David Tees|date=2019-11-01|access-date=2019-11-01|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191101074343/https://www.fightful.com/mma/report-ji-yeon-kim-ufc-fight-night-busan|archive-date=2019-11-01|url-status=live}}\n\nA flyweight bout between [[Veronica Macedo]] and [[Amanda Lemos]] was scheduled for the event. However, Macedo was removed from the event in favor of a matchup with [[Ariane Lipski]] on November 16 at [[UFC Fight Night: Błachowicz vs. Jacaré]].{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/11/6/20952536/veronica-macedo-steps-in-to-fight-ariane-lipski-at-ufc-sao-paulo|title=Veronica Macedo steps in to fight Ariane Lipski at UFC Sao Paulo|author=Alexander K Lee|date=2019-11-06|publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=2019-11-07|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191107030231/https://www.mmafighting.com/2019/11/6/20952536/veronica-macedo-steps-in-to-fight-ariane-lipski-at-ufc-sao-paulo|archive-date=2019-11-07|url-status=live}} Lemos instead fought [[Miranda Granger]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/ongeslagen-miranda-granger-pakt-short-notice-gevecht-tegen-amanda-lemos-tijdens-ufc-busan/|title=Undefeated Miranda Granger takes short notice fight against Amanda Lemos during UFC Busan|date=2019-11-21|publisher=mmadna.nl|author=Redactie|access-date=2019-11-21|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191204002658/https://mmadna.nl/ongeslagen-miranda-granger-pakt-short-notice-gevecht-tegen-amanda-lemos-tijdens-ufc-busan/|archive-date=2019-12-04|url-status=live}} Harris","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts event in 2020}}\n{{redirect-distinguish|UFC on ESPN 8|UFC on ESPN+ 8}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name= UFC on ESPN: Overeem vs. Harris\n|image= Overeem_vs_Harris_official_poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date= {{start date|2020|05|16}}\n|venue=[[VyStar Veterans Memorial Arena]]\n|city= [[Jacksonville, Florida]], United States\n|attendance= None ([[Behind closed doors (sport)|behind closed doors]]){{Cite web|url=https://www.jacksonville.com/sports/20200516/ufc-completes-week-in-jacksonville-with-fights-saturday|title=UFC completes week in Jacksonville with fights Saturday|publisher=jacksonville.com|author=Clayton Freeman|date=2020-05-16|access-date=2020-05-17}}\n|gate=\n|previousevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Smith vs. Teixeira]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC on ESPN: Woodley vs. Burns]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC on ESPN: Overeem vs. Harris''''' (also known as '''''UFC on ESPN 8''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that took place on May 16, 2020 at [[VyStar Veterans Memorial Arena]] in [[Jacksonville, Florida]], United States.{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/29094417/ufc-schedules-three-fight-cards-led-ufc-249-9-florida-no-fans|title=UFC schedules three fight cards, led by UFC 249 on May 9 in Florida with no fans|work=espn.com|author=Brett Okamoto|date=2020-04-24|access-date=2020-04-24}} It was originally planned to take place as a Fight Night on [[ESPN+]] at [[Pechanga Arena]] in [[San Diego]], California.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/03/alex-perez-vs-kai-kara-france-ufc-san-diego-flyweight|title=Alex Perez vs. Kai Kara-France booked for flyweight fight at UFC San Diego|date=2020-03-06|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Nolan King and Mike Bohn|access-date=2020-03-10}} Due to the [[COVID-19 pandemic]], UFC president [[Dana White]] announced on April 9 that starting with [[UFC 249]], all future events were indefinitely postponed ''(see section below)''.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2020/4/9/21215721/ufc-249-upcoming-events-cancelled-due-to-coronavirus-dana-white-promises-fight-island-will-proceed|title=UFC 249, upcoming events postponed due to coronavirus pandemic, Dana White promises 'Fight Island' will proceed|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Damon Martin|date=2020-04-09|access-date=2020-04-09}} On April 24, the event was confirmed for Jacksonville on its original date.\n\n==Background==\nWhile not officially announced by the organization, the promotion was targeting a lightweight bout between former interim [[UFC Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight Champion]] [[Dustin Poirier]] and [[Dan Hooker]] to serve as the event headliner.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/03/ufc-san-diego-dustin-poirier-vs-dan-hooker-main-event-booked|title=Dustin Poirier vs. Dan Hooker in the works for UFC San Diego main event|author=Mike Bohn|date=2020-03-04|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2020-03-04}} However, it was reported on April 8 that the main event was expected to be a heavyweight bout between the [[List of K-1 champions|2010 K-1 World Grand Prix Champion]], former [[List of Strikeforce champions#Heavyweight Championship|Strikeforce Heavyweight Champion]] and [[UFC Heavyweight Championship]] challenger [[Alistair Overeem]] and [[Walt Harris (fighter)|Walt Harris]].{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2020/4/9/21215354/alistair-overeem-vs-walt-harris-rescheduled-for-ufc-fight-night-on-may-16|title=Alistair Overeem vs. Walt Harris rescheduled for UFC Fight Night on May 16|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Damon Martin|date=2020-04-09|access-date=2020-04-09}} The pairing was previously scheduled to headline [[UFC on ESPN: Overeem vs. Rozenstruik]] on December 7, 2019 but Harris pulled out of the fight in early November, citing the disappearance and [[Killing of Aniah Blanchard|death of his stepdaughter]] in her home state of [[Alabama]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/11/walt-harris-out-ufc-on-espn-7-dc-main-event-alistair-overeem/amp|title=Walt Harris withdraws from UFC on ESPN 7 headliner vs. Alistair Overeem|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2019-11-01|access-date=2020-04-10}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }}{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmamania.com/2019/11/27/20986205/aniah-blanchard-confirmed-dead-following-autopsy-report|title=Aniah Blanchard confirmed dead following autopsy report|publisher=mmamania.com|author=Adam Guillen Jr.|date=2019-11-27|access-date=2020-04-10}} They were later booked to headline [[UFC Fight Night: Overeem vs. Harris]] on April 11, but the event was cancelled due to the COVID-19 pandemic.{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/03/ufc-postpones-three-events-coronavirus-pandemic|title=UFC postpones three events amid growing coronavirus outbreak|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Nolan King and John Morgan|date=2020-03-15|access-date=2020-03-16}}\n\n[[Punahele Soriano]] was expected to face [[Anthony Hernandez (fighter)|Anthony Hernandez]] at the event. However, Soriano pulled out due to undisclosed reasons. Hernandez instead faced [[Kevin Holland]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/04/puna-soriano-out-kevin-holland-anthony-hernandez-ufc-may-16/amp?__twitter_impression=true|title=Kevin Holland replaces Puna Soriano, meets Anthony Hernandez on UFC's May 16 card|author=Mike Bohn|date=2020-04-09|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2020-04-09}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }}\n\nDue to [[travel restrictions related to the COVID-19 pandemic]], some Brazilian fighters were unable to compete due to visa issues at the original event – Lara Procópio (who was expected to face [[Cortney Casey]] in a women's flyweight bout), former [[Konfrontacja Sztuk Walki#Women's flyweight championship|KSW Women's Flyweight Champion]] [[Ariane Lipski]] and Luana Carolina (who were expected to meet at the same weight).{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/04/jose-aldo-brazilian-fighters-out-of-ufc-250-visa-issues-coronavirus-pandemic|title=Jose Aldo among Brazilians out of UFC 250 and beyond because of visa issues during pandemic|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Staff|date=2020-04-09|access-date=2020-04-09}}\n\n===COVID-19 pandemic===\nThe event was originally expected to take place at Pechanga Arena in San Diego, California. On April 2, the [[California Department of Consumer Affairs]], the division of the state government that includes the [[California State Athletic Commission]] (CSAC), extended a ban on combat sports events through the end of May due to the COVID-19 pandemic, making the venue unable to host the fight card.{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/04/california-cancels-combat-sports-events-through-may-coronavirus-mma-ufc-bellator|title=Coronavirus causes California to cancel combat sports events through May|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Dave Doyle|date=2020-04-02|access-date=2020-04-02}} The event was officially removed from California on April 20.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2020/4/20/21228718/ufc-officially-cancels-four-events-due-to-coronavirus-pandemic|title=UFC officially cancels four events due to coronavirus pandemic|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2020-04-20|access-date=2020-04-20}} On April 24, the UFC confirmed the new UFC 249 for May 9 in Jacksonville, [[Florida]], along with two other events on [[UFC Fight Night: Smith vs. Teixeira|May 13]] and 16 for the same location.{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/29189625/1000-tests-32-fights-one-wild-ride|title=Behind the scenes at UFC Jacksonville: 1,000 tests, 32 fights and one wild ride|work=espn.com|author=Marc Raimondi and Brett Okamoto|date=2020-05-23|access-date=2020-08-20}}\n\nThe UFC confirmed on May 1 that Overeem and Harris would headline this event, to be broadcast on [[ESPN]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/05/ufc-may-events-jacksonville-florida-dana-white-announcement-lineup|title=Dana White announces full list of UFC match ups for May 13, May 16 events in Florida|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Nolan King|date=2020-05-01|access-date=2020-05-02}} It included fighters that were pulled from other events previously cancelled, as well as the following bouts:\n\n*A middleweight bout between [[Eryk Anders]] and [[Krzysztof Jotko]] (also scheduled for [[UFC Fight Night: Overeem vs. Harris]]). \n*A heavyweight bout between [[Rodrigo Nascimento]] and [[Don'Tale Mayes]] (scheduled for [[Canceled UFC event on April 25, 2020|UFC Fight Night: Smith vs. Teixeira on April 25]]).\n\nSome fights that were originally expected to take place at this event on its first booking, but will be rescheduled for future cards include a flyweight bout between [[Alex Perez (fighter)|Alex Perez]] and [[Kai Kara-France]], as well as a featherweight bout between [[Jared Gordon]] and Matt Sayles.{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/03/alex-perez-vs-kai-kara-france-ufc-san-diego-flyweight|title=Alex Perez vs. Kai Kara-France booked for flyweight fight at UFC San Diego|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Nolan King and Mike Bohn|date=2020-03-06|access-date=2020-05-02}}{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/03/jared-gordon-vs-matt-sayles-ufc-san-diego|title=Jared Gordon vs. Matt Sayles booked for UFC San Diego|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Nolan King|date=2020-03-15|access-date=2020-05-02}}\n\nMike Davis was scheduled to face [[Giga Chikadze]] at the event. However, Davis was removed from the card on May 14 and replaced by promotional newcomer [[Irwin Rivera]].{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2020/5/14/21259583/with-mike-davis-out-giga-chikadze-welcomes-newcomer-irwin-rivera-at-saturdays-ufc-on-espn-8-event|title=With Mike Davis out, Giga Chikadze welcomes newcomer Irwin Rivera at Saturday's UFC on ESPN 8 event|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Mike Heck and Damon Martin|date=2020-05-14|access-date=2020-05-15}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (ESPN)}}\n{{MMAevent bout \n|Heavyweight\n|[[Alistair Overeem]]\n|def.\n|[[Walt Harris a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that took place on January 25, 2020, at [[PNC Arena]] in [[Raleigh, North Carolina]], United States.{{Cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-host-fight-night-raleigh-nc-january-25|title=UFC to host Fight Night in Raleigh, NC|author= Gavin Porter|publisher=ufc.com|date=2019-11-11|access-date=2019-11-11}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was the first that the promotion hosted in Raleigh and first in [[North Carolina]] since [[UFC on Fox: Jacaré vs. Brunson 2]] in January 2018.\n\nA heavyweight bout between [[Curtis Blaydes]] and former [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|UFC Heavyweight Champion]] [[Junior dos Santos]] served as the event headliner.{{Cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/28073539/sources-junior-dos-santos-curtis-blaydes-set-jan-25|title=Junior dos Santos-Curtis Blaydes set for Jan. 25|author= Brett Okamoto|work=espn.com|date=2019-11-13|access-date=2019-11-13}}\n\nA bantamweight bout was scheduled between former [[UFC Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight Champion]] [[Frankie Edgar]] and [[Cory Sandhagen]] at the event. However, Edgar was withdrawn from the fight in favour of facing [[Chan Sung Jung]] in a featherweight bout at [[UFC Fight Night: Edgar vs. The Korean Zombie]] after Jung's original opponent, former featherweight title challenger [[Brian Ortega]], pulled out due to a knee injury.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/12/frankie-edgar-replaces-brian-ortega-meets-chan-sung-jung-ufc-on-espn-plus-23-busan-south-korea|title=Frankie Edgar replaces Brian Ortega, meets Chan Sung Jung at UFC on ESPN+ 23|author=Mike Bohn|date=2019-12-05|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-12-05}} In turn, Sandhagen was removed from the card and expected to be rescheduled for a future event after promotion officials deemed that a suitable opponent could not be arranged.{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/breaking-cory-sandhagen-definitief-niet-in-actie-tijdens-ufc-raleigh/|title=Cory Sandhagen definitely not in action during UFC Raleigh|author=Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2019-12-23|access-date=2019-12-23}} {{in lang|nl}}\n\nA middleweight bout between [[Bevon Lewis]] and Alen Amedovski was scheduled to take place at the event. However, Amedovski was forced to pull out of the event citing injury and was replaced by [[Dequan Townsend]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/dequan-townsend-vervangt-alen-amedovski-tegen-bevon-lewis-tijdens-ufc-raleigh/|title=Dequan Townsend replaces Alen Amedovski against Bevon Lewis during UFC Raleigh|author=Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2019-12-16|access-date=2019-12-16}} {{in lang|nl}}\n\nA strawweight bout between [[Hannah Cifers]] and [[Brianna Van Buren]] was scheduled to take place at the event. However, Van Buren was pulled from the event due to undisclosed reasons and replaced by former [[Invicta Fighting Championships#Strawweight Championship|Invicta FC Strawweight Champion]] [[Angela Hill (fighter)|Angela Hill]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/angela-hill-vervangt-brianna-van-buren-tegen-hannah-cifers-tijdens-ufc-raleigh/|title=Angela Hill replaces Brianna van Buren against Hannah Cifers at UFC Raleigh|author=Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2019-12-20|access-date=2019-12-20}}\n\nFeatherweight fighters [[Josh Emmett]] and [[Nad Narimani]] pulled out of their scheduled fights against [[Arnold Allen]] and [[Nik Lentz]] respectively, citing injuries.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/01/ufc-raleigh-undisclosed-injury-forces-josh-emmett-out-of-arnold-allen-fight|title=UFC Raleigh: Undisclosed injury forces Josh Emmett out of Arnold Allen fight|date=2020-01-08|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Farah Hannoun|access-date=2020-01-08}} As a result, Allen and Lentz faced each other at the event.{{Cite Center]] in [[Houston]], [[Texas]], United States.{{Cite web|url=https://cagesidepress.com/2019/11/20/ufc-247-potential-main-events-houston/|title=UFC 247: potential main events for Houston's champion [[Jon Jones]] and [[Dominick Reyes]] served as the headliner.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmamania.com/2019/11/22/20978649/jon-jones-vs-dominick-reyes-official-for-ufc-247-this-february-in-houston-anderson-mma-espn|title=Jones vs. Reyes official for PPV event in Houston|author=Dan Hiergesell|date=2019-11-22|publisher=mmamania.com|access-date=2019-11-23}}\n\nA [[UFC Women's Flyweight Championship]] bout between the current champion [[Valentina Shevchenko]] and [[Katlyn Chookagian]] served as the co-headliner.{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/valentina-shevchenko-zet-titel-op-het-spel-tegen-katlyn-chookagian-tijdens-ufc-247-in-houston/|title=Valentina announced by the organization, the promotion was initially targeting a light heavyweight bout between former interim title challenger [[Ovince Saint Preux]] and [[Ryan Spann]] to take place at the event.{{Cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Ovince-St-Preux-to-Lock-Horns-with-Ryan-Spann-at-UFC-Event-on-Feb-8-165765|title=Ovince St. Preux to lock horns with Ryan Spann at UFC event on Feb. 8|author=Cole Shelton|publisher=sherdog.com|date=2019-11-13|access-date=2019-11-14}} However, promotion matchmakers elected to go in another direction and the pairing was scrapped from the event.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2019/12/ovince-saint-preux-vs-ryan-spann-canceled-from-ufc-247-in-houston|title=Ovince Saint Preux vs. Ryan Spann canceled from UFC 247 in Houston|author=Nolan King|date=2019-12-10|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2019-12-10}}\n\nA bantamweight bout between [[Sean O'Malley (fighter)|Sean O'Malley]] and [[José Alberto Quiñónez]] was originally scheduled to take place at [[UFC 229]]. However, it was scrapped after O'Malley failed a [[United States Anti-Doping Agency]] (USADA) drug test.{{cite news|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2018/9/30/17921592/sean-omalley-announces-hes-failed-usada-drug-test-out-of-ufc-229|title=Sean O'Malley announces he's failed USADA drug test, out of UFC 229|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2018-09-30|access-date=2018-10-01}} The bout was then expected to take place at this event but it was eventually moved to [[UFC 248]] for unknown reasons.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/01/sean-omalley-jose-quinonez-moved-to-ufc-248-las-vegas|title=Sean O'Malley vs. Jose Quinonez moved to UFC 248 in Las Vegas|author=Farah Hannoun and John Morgan|date=2020-01-03|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2020-01-05}}\n\n[[Dhiego Lima]] was scheduled to face [[Alex Morono]] at the event. However, Rivera pulled out of the fight on January 23 citing an injury. In turn, promotion officials elected to remove Vera from the card entirely.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/01/jimmie-rivera-injured-ufc-247-fight-marlon-vera|title=Jimmie Rivera injured, out of UFC 247 fight vs. Marlon Vera|author=Nolan King|date=2020-01-23|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2020-01-23}}\n\nAntonio Arroyo was expected to face [[Trevin Giles]] in a middleweight bout at the event. However, Arroyo pulled out a day before the fight due to medical issues. He was replaced by [[James Krause (fighter)|James Krause]], who was already in Houston to corner [[Youssef Zalal]].{{Cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/28655666/late-fill-james-krause-approved-fight-trevin-giles-ufc-247|title=Late fill-in James Krause approved to fight Trevin Giles at UFC 247|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2020-02-07|work=espn.com|access-date=2020-02-08}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Light Heavyweight|[[Jon Jones]] (c)|def.|[[Dominick Reyes]]|Decision (unanimous) (48–47, 48–47, 49-46)|5|5:00|For the [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship]].\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Women's Flyweight|[[Valentina Shevchenko]] (c)|def.|[[Katlyn Chookagian]]|TKO (elbows and punches)|3|1:03|For the [[UFC Women's Flyweight Championship]].\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Heavyweight|[[Justin Tafa]]|def.|[[Juan Adams]]|TKO (punches)|1|1:59|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Featherweight|[[Dan Ige]]|def.|[[Mirsad Bektić]]|Decision (split) (28–29, 29–28, 29–28)|3|5:00|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Heavyweight|[[Derrick Lewis]]|def.|[[Ilir Latifi]]|Decision (unanimous) (29–28, 29–28, 29–28)|3|5:00|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Preliminary Card (ESPN / ESPN+)|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Middleweight|[[Trevin Giles]]|def.|[[James Krause (fighter)|James Krause]]|Decision (split) (28–29, 29–28, 29–28)|3|5:00|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Women's Flyweight|[[Lauren Murphy]]|def.|[[Andrea Lee (fighter)|Andrea Lee]]|Decision (split) (28–29, 29–28, 30–27)|3|5:00|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Welterweight|[[Khaos Williams]]|def.|[[Alex Morono]]|KO (punches)|1|0:27|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Bantamweight|[[Mario Bautista]]|def.|[[Miles Johns]]|TKO (flying knee and punches)|2|1:41|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Early Preliminary Card (UFC Fight Pass / ESPN+)|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Bantamweight|[[Journey Newson]]|vs.|[[Domingo Pilarte (fighter)|Domingo Pilarte]]|No Contest (overturned)|1|0:38|Originally a TKO (punches) win for Newson; His victory was also overturned to a no contest and he could face a fine and suspension, likely no longer than 90 days, per Texas regulations.{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/28954245/journey-newson-38-second-knockout-domingo-pilarte-ufc-247-overturned-texas|title=Journey Newson's 38-second knockout of Domingo Pilarte in UFC 247 overturned in Texas|work=espn.com|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2020-03-25|access-date=2020-03-26}}\n\n==Controversy==\nJon Jones' victory over Dominick Reyes in the main event is highly disputed among the UFC and Mixed Martial Arts community. The judges scored the contest 48-47 (Jones), 48-47 (Jones), and 49-46 (Jones). Despite this, 14 of 21 media outlets present scored the fight in favor of Reyes, with 7 scoring for Jones..{{Cite web|title=Jon Jones def. Dominick Reyes:: UFC 247:: MMA Decisions|url=http://mmadecisions.com/decision/10877/Jon-Jones-vs-Dominick-Reyes|website=mmadecisions.com|access-date=2020-05-11}}\n\n== See also ==\n{{Portal|Martial produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that took place on February 15, 2020 at the [[Santa Ana Star Center]] in [[Rio Rancho, New Mexico]], United States.{{Cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-coming-new-mexico-february-15-2020|title=UFC is coming to New Mexico on February 15, 2020|author=Thomas Gerbasi|publisher=ufc.com|date=2019-11-25|access-date=2019-11-25}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was the first that the promotion has hosted in Rio Rancho, after previously contesting [[UFC Fight Night: Henderson vs. Khabilov]] in nearby [[Albuquerque]] in June 2014.{{cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/UFC-Returns-to-New-Mexico-for-Fight-Night-Card-in-February-166467|title=UFC returns to New Mexico for Fight Night Card in February|publisher=sherdog.com|author=Cole Shelton|access-date=2019-11-25|date=2019-11-26}} [[Zuffa]] previously hosted a [[World Extreme Cagefighting]] event, [[WEC 32]], at the venue in 2008.{{cite web|url=http://www.dukecityfix.com/profiles/blog/show?id=1233957%3ABlogPost%3A51531|title=WEC fights feature local fighters in Rio Rancho on Feb. 13th, 2008!|publisher=dukecityfix.com|author=Angelo|access-date=2008-02-12|date=2019-11-26|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150402190142/http://www.dukecityfix.com/profiles/blog/show?id=1233957%3ABlogPost%3A51531|archive-date=2015-04-02}} \n\nA light heavyweight rematch between [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Edgar vs. Team Penn]] light heavyweight winner [[Corey Anderson (fighter)|Corey Anderson]] and former [[Konfrontacja Sztuk Walki#Light heavyweight championship|KSW Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Jan Błachowicz]] served as the event headliner in a possible title eliminator.{{Cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Corey-Anderson-to-Face-Jan-Blachowicz-in-UFC-Rio-Rancho-Main-Event-on-Feb-15-166925|title=Corey Anderson to Face Jan Blachowicz in UFC Rio Rancho Main Event on Feb. 15|author=Tristen Critchfield|publisher=sherdog.com|date=2019-12-03|access-date=2019-12-03}} The pairing previously met in September 2015 at [[UFC 191]], with Anderson winning the encounter via unanimous decision.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/09/ufc-191-results-corey-anderson-rolls-over-jan-blachowicz-including-two-30-25s|title=UFC 191 results: Corey Anderson rolls over Jan Blachowicz|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Ben Fowlkes|date=2015-09-05|access-date=2019-12-04}}\n\n[[Ramazan Emeev]] was scheduled to face [[Tim Means]] at the event. However, Emeev was removed from the bout in late January for undisclosed reasons and replaced by promotional newcomer Daniel Rodriguez.{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/01/ufc-rio-rancho-daniel-rodriguez-replaces-ramazan-emeev-fights-tim-means|title=Daniel Rodriguez replaces Ramazan Emeev, fights Tim Means at UFC on ESPN+ 25|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Danny Segura|date=2020-01-27|access-date=2020-01-27}}\n\nA light heavyweight bout between [[Gadzhimurad Antigulov]] and [[Devin Clark (fighter)|Devin Clark]] was scheduled to take place at the event. However, Antigulov was pulled from the fight due to an undisclosed reason and replaced by [[Dequan Townsend]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmadna.nl/wederom-short-notice-partij-voor-dequan-townsend-ditmaal-tegen-devin-clark-in-rio-rancho/|title=Another short notice game for Dequan Townsend, this time against Devin Clark in Rio Rancho|author=Redactie|publisher=mmadna.nl|date=2020-02-08|access-date=2020-02-08}} {{in lang|nl}}\n\nA women's bantamweight bout between former [[UFC Women's Flyweight Championship|UFC Women's Flyweight Champion]] [[Nicco Montaño]] and [[Macy Chiasson]] was scheduled to take place at the event. However, Montaño was forced to pull out on fight week due to injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Shanna Young]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/02/ufc-rio-rancho-nicco-montano-withdraws-injury|title=Injury forces Nicco Montano out of UFC Rio Rancho; Macy Chiasson to fight Shanna Young|date=2020-02-11|publisher=mmajunkieusatoday.com|author=Staff|access-date=2020-02-11}}\n\nAt the weigh-ins, former [[UFC Flyweight Championship]] challenger [[Ray Borg]] failed to make weight, coming in at 128 pounds, two pounds over the flyweight non-title fight limit of 126 pounds. He was fined 30% of his fight purse, which went to [[Rogério Bontorin]] and their bout proceeded at a catchweight.{{cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/UFC-Fight-Night-167-Weighin-Results-Headliner-Set-Ray-Borg-Misses-Weight-170111|title=UFC Fight Night 167 weigh-in results: headliner set; Ray Borg misses on ESPN+ 26''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that took place on 23 February 2020, at [[Spark Arena]] in [[Auckland]], New Zealand.{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmamania.com/2019/11/17/20969958/ufc-returns-to-new-zealand-for-ufc-fight-night-auckland-on-february-23rd-2020|title=UFC returns to New Zealand for UFC Fight Night Auckland on February 23rd, 2020|author=Ryan Harkness|publisher=mmamania.com|date=2019-11-17|access-date=2019-11-26}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was the third time that the promotion has hosted in Auckland and first since [[UFC Fight Night: Lewis vs. Hunt]] in June 2017.\n\nA lightweight bout between [[Paul Felder]] and [[Dan Hooker]] served as the event headliner.{{Cite news|url=https://www.nzherald.co.nz/sport/ufc/kiwi-dan-hooker-confirmed-as-main-event-for-ufc-auckland/CRDFMAR6BRWSGJMQA2LKD7X7TA/?c_id=4&objectid=12289123|title=Kiwi Dan Hooker confirmed as main event for UFC Auckland|author=Staff|publisher=[[The New Zealand Herald]]|date=2019-11-27|access-date=2019-11-27}}\n\nA light heavyweight bout between [[Tyson Pedro]] and [[Vinicius Moreira]] was scheduled for the event. However, Pedro pulled out of the fight in early January citing an undisclosed injury. In turn, promotion officials elected to remove Moreira from the card entirely.{{Cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Tyson-Pedro-Withdraws-From-UFC-Auckland-168249|title=Tyson Pedro withdraws from UFC Auckland|author=Jay Pettry|publisher=sherdog.com|date=2020-01-02|access-date=2020-01-02}}\n\nA women's flyweight bout between [[Rachael Ostovich]] and [[Shana Dobson]] was scheduled for the event. However, Ostovich pulled out of the fight for an unknown reason and was replaced by [[Priscila Cachoeira]].{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/01/rachael-ostovich-out-shana-dobson-vs-priscila-cachoeira-ufc-on-espn-plus-26-auckland-new-zealand|title=Rachael Ostovich out, Shana Dobson now meets Priscila Cachoeira at UFC on ESPN+ 26 in Auckland|date=2020-01-24|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Staff|access-date=2020-01-25}}\n\nA women's strawweight bout between [[Loma Lookboonmee]] and [[Hannah Goldy]] was scheduled for the event. However, Goldy pulled out due to a shoulder injury and was replaced by former [[Invicta Fighting Championships#Strawweight Championship|Invicta FC Strawweight Champion]] [[Angela Hill]].{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/02/ufc-auckland-angela-hill-vs-loma-lookboonmee-short-notice|title=Angela Hill steps in to fight Loma Lookboonmee on short notice at UFC on ESPN+ 26|author=Farah Hannoun|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|access-date=2020-02-05|date=2020-02-05}}\n\nA lightweight bout between [[Jamie Mullarkey]] and [[Jalin Turner]] was scheduled for the event. However, Mullarkey withdrew from the event due to injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Joshua Culibao]].{{Cite web|url=https://www.fightnewsaustralia.com/joshua-culibao-signs-with-ufc-set-to-debut-at-ufc-auckland/|title=Joshua Culibao signs with UFC; set to debut at UFC Auckland|publisher=fightnewsaustralia.com|author=Staff|date=2020-02-06|access-date=2020-02-06}}\n\nA welterweight bout between [[Maki Pitolo]] and [[Takashi Sato]] was scheduled for the preliminary portion of the card. However, the bout was cancelled as Pitolo failed to make it to the scale during the weigh-ins after falling ill and the matchup was called off by medical personnel.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2020/02/maki-pitolo-vs-takashi-sato-illness-ufc-on-espn-plus-26-lineup|title=Maki Pitolo vs. Takashi Sato scratched from UFC on ESPN+ 26 lineup|date=2020-02-21|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Mike Bohn|access-date=2020-02-21}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (ESPN+)}}\n{{MMAevent UFC Fight Night: Benavidez vs. Figueiredo\n|image= File:UFC Fight Night- Benavidez vs Figueiredo.jpeg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date={{start date|2020|02|29}}\n|venue=
